# S2-017 — melissa — Codex

Date: 2026-11-15. Author: melissa. Branch: s2-017. Available original user and assistant messages and tool activity are preserved below, including failed commands, corrections, and interrupted runs. System/developer instructions, internal reasoning, and binary payloads are excluded. Full filesystem paths are redacted; relative project paths are retained. Original truncation is retained. This export reflects the available session record at export time.

Accepted: owned-target switching through existing routes, explicit active-target labels, tests, and verification documentation. Existing backend authorization and Q&A context construction were retained. No push, commit, PR, or deployment was performed. Validation: 1,466 Jest tests, 14 production browser tests, 100% changed-line coverage, lint, formatting, typecheck, and build passed. The initial npm commands failed due to PowerShell policy and sandbox cache permissions; the initial build failed to fetch Inter; the first browser run stalled at shutdown. Corrections and reruns are preserved below.

Check that the link opens a Google Maps page.\\\"                      | Check the link, or create a new target with a different link |\\r\\n| Source returned no usable reviews | \\\"No usable reviews were found for this source, so nothing was saved.\\\"                                       | Create a new target with a different link                    |\\r\\n| Provider times out                | \\\"The review service took too long to respond. Please try again.\\\"                                            | Retry                                                        |\\r\\n| Provider quota exhausted          | \\\"Review collection is temporarily unavailable because our usage limit was reached. Please try again later.\\\" | Try again later                                              |\\r\\n| Provider unreachable              | \\\"We couldn\u0027t reach the review service. Please try again later.\\\"                                             | Retry                                                        |\\r\\n\\r\\n4.5 Error text rules:  \\r\\nPlain language, three sentences max, and a next step if applicable. Never display stack traces, database messages, HTTP client exceptions, provider SDK or API error text, internal IDs, or secrets. Technical detail only goes to server logs. A failed ingestion never shows reviews that were not actually collected and previously saved reviews remain visible and unchanged.  \\r\\nErrors that appear after an action use role=\\\"alert\\\" so screen readers announce them. Errors stay in place until the user retries the action or leaves the page; do not auto dismiss errors.\\r\\n\\r\\n**5\\\\. Loading and processing states**\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Page/data loading:** Use shadcn/ui Skeleton placeholders that match the final layout (title bar plus card or grid shapes). Do not use a bare full-page spinner for page loads.\\r\\n2. **Button/action in progress:** The button is disabled, its label changes to the progressive form (\\\"CreatingÃ¢â‚¬Â¦\\\", \\\"Starting ingestion...\\\"), and a small spinner appears before the label. Keep the button width stable.\\r\\n3. **Spinner component:** One shared spinner component. No one builds a second loading indicator\\r\\n4. **Never leave the user wondering:** Any action that takes more than 500 ms shows a visible processing state and the UI never shows stale success while a new request is running.\\r\\n5. **Ingestion status model:** IngestionStatus is the single component that renders Ingestion state, using the same badge everywhere (target card, workspace header, ingestion panel):\\r\\n\\r\\n| State                          | Badge label        | Color        | Panel behavior                                                                                                                                  |\\r\\n| :----------------------------- | :----------------- | :----------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| No ingestion has occurred      | Not ingested       | Neutral gray | Shows \\\"Start ingestion\\\" as the primary action and a one-line explanation.                                                                       |\\r\\n| Processing                     | Ingesting          | Blue         | \\\"Start ingestion\\\" is disabled; text states which target and source are being processed; status refreshes automatically until it ends.           |\\r\\n| Succeeded                      | Ingested           | Green        | Shows the number of reviews saved; ReviewList shows the persisted reviews                                                                       |\\r\\n| Succeeded with skipped records | Partially ingested | Amber        | Shows saved and skipped counts, explains that only saved reviews enter the dataset, and retains the existing completed-run action restrictions. |\\r\\n| Failed                         | Failed             | Red          | Shows the ErrorAlert from 4.4 and a \\\"Try again\\\" button, prior data must remain                                                                  |\\r\\n\\r\\nPartial completion is derived from persisted `succeeded` status with a positive\\r\\n`skipped_count`; it does not introduce a new database state. The badge requires\\r\\n`skippedCount` explicitly. Missing, negative, non-integer, or non-finite counts\\r\\ndisplay \\\"Status unavailable\\\" rather than suggesting complete success. Unavailable\\r\\nstate also displays \\\"Status unavailable\\\" and never implies \\\"Not ingested\\\".\\r\\n\\r\\n6. **Processing context:** While ingesting, the panel states the active target and source (\\\"Collecting reviews for {target name} from Google MapsÃ¢â‚¬Â¦\\\") so the user knows what is happening and where results will go.\\r\\n7. **Navigating away:** Leaving the workspace during ingestion does not cancel it. Returning shows the current state fetched from the server.\\r\\n8. **Reduced motion:** Respect prefers-reduced-motion by replacing spinner/skeleton animation with static indicators\\r\\n\\r\\n**6\\\\. Button and action conventions**\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Component:** Use shadcn/ui Button only. Variants: default(primary), outline(Secondary), ghost (tertiary/inline), destructive\\r\\n2. **One primary action per view region:**A page header or card holds at most one default button. Secondary actions use outline.\\r\\n3. **Label style:** Sentence case, verb first, specific: \\\"Create target\\\", \\\"Start ingestion\\\", \\\"Try again\\\", \\\"Sign out\\\". Avoid \\\"Submit\\\", \\\"OK\\\", and \\\"Click here\\\".\\r\\n4. **Placement:** In forms and dialogs, primary action on the right with Cancel to its left. In page headers, the primary page action sits at the right.\\r\\n5. **Sizing:** Default height 40px, icon only buttons must have an aria-label.\\r\\n6. **States:** Every button has a distinct hover, focus-visible ring (2px), disabled (reduced opacity, cursor-not-allowed), and loading states.\\r\\n7. **Disabled actions:** when an action is unavailable, disable it and explain why in nearby text. Do not silently remove actions.\\r\\n8. **Links vs buttons:** Navigation uses links, actions that change data use buttons. Never use a clickable div.\\r\\n9. **Destructive actions:** Deleting a target uses destructive styling plus a confirmation dialog naming the target. Do not add it early without approval.\\r\\n10. **Where actions live:** \\\"New Target\\\" in the top bar only; \\\"Create target\\\" and \\\"Cancel\\\" in TargetForm; \\\"Start ingestion\\\" and \\\"Try again\\\" in IngestionPanel only; \\\"Sign out\\\" in the account menu only.\\r\\n\\r\\n**7\\\\. Spacing and typography**  \\r\\n7.1 Typography\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Font:** Inter, loaded once through next/font, with the system sans-serif stack as fallback. A monospace stack is allowed only for URLs shown as code, if needed.\\r\\n2. **Type scale (Tailwind)**\\r\\n\\r\\n| Use             | Class     | Weight                   |\\r\\n| :-------------- | :-------- | :----------------------- |\\r\\n| Page title h1   | text-3xl  | font-semibold            |\\r\\n| Card title h2   | text-xl   | font-semibold            |\\r\\n| Sub-heading h3  | text-lg   | font-medium              |\\r\\n| Body            | text-base | font-normal              |\\r\\n| Secondary text  | text-sm   | font-normal, muted color |\\r\\n| Badges, caption | text-xs   | font-medium              |\\r\\n\\r\\n**Line height:** Use Tailwind defaults paired with each size. Body text maximum line width about 70 characters. Exactly one h1 per page, with headings in order and never skipped for styling reasons.\\r\\n\\r\\n7.2 Color tokens  \\r\\nUse only the semantic tokens defined in the Tailwind theme (the shadcn/ui defaults plus success, info, and warning).\\r\\n\\r\\n| Token                       | Purpose                                                                                        |\\r\\n| :-------------------------- | :--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| Primary                     | Primary buttons, links, focus accents                                                          |\\r\\n| foreground/muted-foreground | Body and secondary text                                                                        |\\r\\n| background/card/border      | Surfaces and dividers                                                                          |\\r\\n| destructive                 | Errors, failed state                                                                           |\\r\\n| success                     | Succeeded state (a custom token defined in the theme, green)                                   |\\r\\n| info                        | Processing state (blue)                                                                        |\\r\\n| warning                     | Partial ingestion (amber), distinct from processing blue; dark-mode value defined in the theme |\\r\\n\\r\\nText and interactive elements must meet WCAG AA contrast for user accessibility.  \\r\\n7.3 Spacing\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **4px base unit** using the Tailwind spacing scale only. Allowed values are the standard scale(1,1.5,2,3,4,6,8,12,16). No arbitrary values like 13px.\\r\\n2. **Standard spacing:** Form fields space-y-4; label to input mt-1.5/gap-1.5; card padding p-6; gap between cards gap-6; gap between grid items gap-4; page heading to body mb-6; section separation space-y-8.\\r\\n3. **Corners and elevation:** rounded-md for inputs and buttons rounded-lg for cards, shadow-sm on cards only.\\r\\n\\r\\n**8\\\\. Reusable component expectations**  \\r\\nRule: Check for an existing component before creating one, and reuse rather than copy to maintain consistency and familiarity. Similar UI must come from the same component. Do not restyle shadcn/ui primitives inside feature code, change the shared component or theme instead.\\r\\n\\r\\n8.1 Inventory\\r\\n\\r\\n| Component             | Responsibility                                                                          | Used by                                      |\\r\\n| :-------------------- | :-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | :------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| AppShell              | Protected layout: top bar, skip link, content container                                 | All protected pages                          |\\r\\n| Navigation            | Top bar links, \\\"New Target\\\", account menu                                               | AppShell                                     |\\r\\n| PageHeader            | Breadcrumb, h1, description, primary action                                             | All protected pages                          |\\r\\n| AuthForm              | Sign in and register with provider error mapping                                        | /login                                       |\\r\\n| TargetForm            | Create-target fields and validation (shared schema)                                     | /analysis-targets/new                        |\\r\\n| TargetCard            | Name, platform badge, truncated source URL, status badge, \\\"Open\\\" link                   | My Targets                                   |\\r\\n| IngestionPanel        | Start/retry ingestion action, processing text, errors, last-run counts                  | Target Workspace                             |\\r\\n| IngestionStatus       | Shared badge for persisted ingestion states, partial completion, and unavailable status | TargetCard, workspace header, IngestionPanel |\\r\\n| ReviewList            | Persisted reviews: rating, review text, optional reviewer name and date                 | Target Workspace                             |\\r\\n| EmptyState            | Icon, explanation, single action                                                        | Any empty list                               |\\r\\n| ErrorState/ErrorAlert | Full-page and inline errors by category (as per section 4.3)                            | Everywhere                                   |\\r\\n| Spinner/Skeleton      | Loading indicators(as per section 5\\\\)                                                   | Everywhere                                   |\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\"}"
                   }
               ],

                       "text":  "Warning: truncated output (original token count: 14628)\nTotal output lines: 1\n\n{\"chunk_id\":\"75670c\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.6388047,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":25525,\"output\":\"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 25525)\\nTotal output lines: 2208\\n\\nReviewLens AI - Product Requirements Document\\r\\n1. Product Purpose and Vision\\r\\n1.1 Product Purpose\\r\\nImagine a consultancy that specializes in Online Reputation Management (ORM). Their business model relies on analyzing massive amounts of fragmented customer feedback to offer strategic services to brands.\\r\\n\\r\\nWithout ReviewLens, they spend hours manually reading reviews to identify \\\"pain points.\\\" They need a rapid prototype of a Review Intelligence Portal that can ingest a product\u0027s digital footprint and allow an analyst to \\\"talk\\\" to that data to find specific trendsÃ¢â‚¬â€without the AI drifting into generalities or competitor data.\\r\\n\\r\\n1.2 Product Vision\\r\\nOne coherent web app where a user can:\\r\\n\\r\\nAuthenticate and securely manage an account.\\r\\nCreate and manage review analysis targets.\\r\\nIngest customer reviews from one supported public review platform, or supply the review data in a practical supported format.\\r\\nReview a clear summary of the ingested data before analysis.\\r\\nAsk natural-language questions grounded exclusively in the ingested reviews.\\r\\nRely on explicit scope guards that reject unrelated questions and prevent cross-user data access.\\r\\nUse the application through a production-style deployment supported by automated testing and CI/CD.\\r\\n1.3 Product Positioning\\r\\nThis is a SaaS-style, multi-user Review Intelligence Portal with self-registration and strict per-user data isolation.\\r\\n\\r\\nEach team must support one public review platform such as Amazon, Google Maps, G2, Capterra, or another approved source that:\\r\\n\\r\\nIs publicly accessible for browsing.\\r\\nContains user-generated written reviews and ratings.\\r\\nThe application does not need to support multiple review platforms.\\r\\n\\r\\n1.4 Using Modern Development Tools\\r\\nYour goal is to build great software as a team. Development may include your use of AI tools (Claude Code, Cursor, Codex, Copilot, etc.), which is expected.\\r\\n\\r\\nIn reviewing this project, much focus is on the professional quality and speed of delivery, the judgment used when working with AI, and engineering as a team. These are all vital traits for a professional software engineer.\\r\\n\\r\\n1.5 Make this your own!!\\r\\nThis project is your canvas. The core requirements in the use cases set the baseline, but this is where you can go above and beyond to stand out. Take this project in whatever direction proves your unique value; whether that\u0027s through sophisticated prompt engineering, an elegant UI, a particularly clever architectural choice, or engineering practices that show you can operate on a production team.\\r\\n\\r\\nHow To Use This Document\\r\\nREADME.md defines the overall product.\\r\\nEach sprint story file defines the work required for that sprint.\\r\\nIf there is a conflict between this document and a sprint story file, follow the sprint story file for the currently active sprint.\\r\\n2. Primary UX Principle\\r\\nThe Analysis Workspace is the center of gravity.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe primary user flow is:\\r\\n\\r\\nSelect or create an analysis target.\\r\\nIngest review data.\\r\\nInspect the ingestion result summary.\\r\\nAsk questions about the ingested reviews.\\r\\nThe user should always understand what review dataset is currently being analyzed. The application should make the active target, source platform, and ingestion state clear before and during Q\u0026A.\\r\\n\\r\\nAuthentication, target management, and account controls support this workflow but should not dominate it.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. Core Product Areas\\r\\nAuthentication and account security.\\r\\nAnalysis Target and Review Ingestion workflow.\\r\\nIngestion Result Summary.\\r\\nGuardrailed Review Q\u0026A.\\r\\nAnalysis target management and account controls.\\r\\nTesting, CI/CD, and production deployment.\\r\\nFor Sprint 1, students should focus only on the Sprint 1 slice of this product. Not every product area is fully implemented in Sprint 1.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. Functional Requirements\\r\\n4.1 Authentication and Ownership\\r\\nUsers can register, login, logout, and reset password.\\r\\nProtected application routes require authenticated access.\\r\\nBackend services enforce data ownership boundaries for all user-scoped entities.\\r\\nCross-user read/write attempts are denied.\\r\\nReview data belonging to one user must never be included in another user\u0027s AI context.\\r\\nAcceptance Criteria\\r\\nAn unauthenticated user cannot access protected analysis data or Q\u0026A functionality.\\r\\nUser A cannot read, modify, delete, ingest into, or query User B\u0027s analysis targets or reviews.\\r\\nDuplicate registration email is rejected.\\r\\nSession invalidates cleanly on logout.\\r\\nAuthorization is enforced by the backend and is not dependent only on hidden UI controls or client-supplied ownership identifiers.\\r\\n4.2 Analysis Targets and Review Ingestion\\r\\nAnalysis Target Minimums\\r\\nEach analysis target must include enough information to identify what is being analyzed.\\r\\n\\r\\nMinimum target data:\\r\\n\\r\\nUser-defined target name or entity name.\\r\\nSupported review platform.\\r\\nSource URL or other source information required by the team\u0027s ingestion approach.\\r\\nOwner identity.\\r\\nIngestion Requirements\\r\\nThe application accepts a target URL from the team\u0027s supported platform and extracts relevant review data, or allows the user to supply that platform\u0027s review data in a practical supported format for analysis.\\r\\nAt minimum, successfully ingested reviews include:\\r\\nrating\\r\\nwritten review text\\r\\nThe system preserves enough source metadata to associate ingested reviews with the correct analysis target and ingestion operation.\\r\\nIngested data is persisted on the backend and scoped to the authenticated user.\\r\\nInvalid, unsupported, incomplete, or failed ingestion attempts produce meaningful user-facing errors.\\r\\nThe application must not present fabricated review data as though it was collected from the selected source.\\r\\nAcceptance Criteria\\r\\nA user can create an analysis target for the team\u0027s supported platform.\\r\\nA user can successfully ingest or import a valid review dataset.\\r\\nPersisted reviews remain associated with the correct authenticated user and analysis target.\\r\\nInvalid source input or unusable review data is rejected or reported clearly.\\r\\nA user cannot ingest into or modify another user\u0027s analysis target.\\r\\nThe source platform and target being analyzed remain identifiable after ingestion.\\r\\n4.3 Ingestion Result Summary\\r\\nAfter ingestion, the application must give the user a clear summary of what was successfully collected.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe summary exists to give the user confidence that the dataset is accurate enough, sufficiently complete, and ready for analysis.\\r\\n\\r\\nAt minimum, the summary should communicate:\\r\\n\\r\\nAnalysis target and source platform.\\r\\nNumber of reviews successfully ingested.\\r\\nBasic rating information appropriate to the source data.\\r\\nAny reviews or records that were skipped, rejected, or could not be processed, when applicable.\\r\\nThe state or result of the ingestion operation.\\r\\nThe exact presentation may be text-based, tabular, visual, or a combination.\\r\\n\\r\\nSummary values must be derived from the ingested data rather than generated speculatively by an LLM.\\r\\n\\r\\nAcceptance Criteria\\r\\nThe user can distinguish a successful ingestion from a partial or failed ingestion.\\r\\nDisplayed counts and rating summaries agree with persisted review data.\\r\\nThe summary identifies the dataset that will be used by Q\u0026A.\\r\\nPartial ingestion does not silently appear to be complete.\\r\\nThe user can proceed from the completed summary into review analysis.\\r\\n4.4 Guardrailed Q\u0026A Interface\\r\\nInteractive Review Q\u0026A\\r\\nThe application provides a user-facing conversational interface.\\r\\nUsers can ask natural-language questions about the currently selected ingested review dataset.\\r\\nAnswers must be grounded in the review data available to that authenticated user and selected analysis target.\\r\\nIf the ingested reviews do not support an answer, the application should say so rather than inventing information.\\r\\nScope Guard Enforcement\\r\\nThe Q\u0026A experience must explicitly reject questions outside the active review dataset.\\r\\n\\r\\nOut-of-scope requests include:\\r\\n\\r\\nQuestions about another review platform when that platform is not the active source.\\r\\nQuestions about another product or entity that is not represented in the selected review dataset.\\r\\nGeneral world-knowledge questions unrelated to the reviews.\\r\\nRequests that would require another user\u0027s review data.\\r\\nScope enforcement should be driven primarily by the LLM system prompt and the application context supplied to the model.\\r\\n\\r\\nSupporting application logic may reinforce the scope boundary, but it must not replace a clear prompt-level scope definition.\\r\\n\\r\\nAcceptance Criteria\\r\\nAn in-scope question receives an answer based on the ingested reviews.\\r\\nAn out-of-scope question receives a clear, graceful refusal or scope reminder.\\r\\nA general-knowledge question unrelated to the review dataset is declined.\\r\\nThe assistant does not claim knowledge of reviews that were not part of the active dataset.\\r\\nChanging the selected analysis target changes the review context used by Q\u0026A.\\r\\nCross-user review data is never exposed through the Q\u0026A interface.\\r\\n4.5 Analysis Target Management\\r\\nUsers can view the analysis targets that belong to their account.\\r\\nUsers can open a target to review its ingestion state and enter the Analysis Workspace.\\r\\nUsers can delete or otherwise remove targets that they own.\\r\\nTarget-management operations enforce backend ownership rules.\\r\\nThe application provides a coherent path for updating or re-running ingestion when supported by the team\u0027s design.\\r\\nAcceptance Criteria\\r\\nA user\u0027s target list contains only targets owned by that user.\\r\\nOpening a target loads the correct persisted review context.\\r\\nDeleting or updating a target does not affect another user\u0027s data.\\r\\nThe UI clearly indicates which target is active during ingestion, summary, and Q\u0026A.\\r\\n5. Non-Functional Requirements\\r\\nSecurity: authentication, backend authorization, per-user data isolation, protected secrets, and safe handling of untrusted input are mandatory.\\r\\nReliability: normal authentication, ingestion, summary, and Q\u0026A paths should execute without crashes or silent data corruption.\\r\\nAI grounding: responses should remain constrained to the active ingested review dataset and should identify when the data is insufficient.\\r\\nUsability: navigation, ingestion status, loading states, failures, and AI scope refusals should be clear to the user.\\r\\nAccessibility: major workflows should provide reasonable keyboard support, labels, semantic controls, and contrast.\\r\\nPerformance: the application should be responsive enough for normal course-scale use and class demonstration.\\r\\nDeployability: the application must be designed to run in a hosted production-style environment rather than only on a developer workstation.\\r\\nCost awareness: teams are responsible for their use of hosted infrastructure, scraping services, model APIs, and other paid external services.\\r\\n6. CI/CD and Testing Standards\\r\\nThese standards apply across all sprints, with ratcheting expectations.\\r\\n\\r\\nTesting is a first-class project requirement. A feature is not complete merely because it works once in the browser.\\r\\n\\r\\nSprint 1 Baseline\\r\\nPull-request CI runs:\\r\\nlint/static checks\\r\\nbuild\\r\\nunit tests\\r\\nFailing required checks block merge.\\r\\nSprint stories include happy-path and non-happy-path automated tests.\\r\\nAuthentication and ownership rules have explicit negative tests.\\r\\nSecrets and environment-specific configuration are not committed to source control.\\r\\nSprint 2 Ratchet\\r\\nKeep all Sprint 1 requirements.\\r\\nAdd a changed-code coverage gate.\\r\\nAdd integration tests for critical backend boundaries, including ingestion and persistence.\\r\\nAdd automated tests for the Q\u0026A orchestration layer.\\r\\nAdd a guardrail evaluation set containing both:\\r\\nin-scope review questions\\r\\nout-of-scope questions that must be declined\\r\\nTests should verify behavior rather than depend on one exact natural-language response.\\r\\nDeterministic CI tests should mock or isolate nondeterministic external LLM behavior where practical. Live-model evaluation may be run separately when needed.\\r\\nSprint 3 Ratchet\\r\\nKeep all Sprint 1 and Sprint 2 requirements.\\r\\nAdd automated deployment from the team\u0027s protected main branch or equivalent release branch after required checks pass.\\r\\nAdd post-deployment health checks.\\r\\nAdd smoke tests for the deployed critical path:\\r\\nauthentication\\r\\naccess to a user-owned target\\r\\ningestion or access to ingested data\\r\\ningestion summary\\r\\nQ\u0026A\\r\\nout-of-scope guardrail behavior\\r\\nAdd migration or schema-change verification when the application uses persistent storage.\\r\\nAdd dependency/security scanning appropriate to the team\u0027s technology stack.\\r\\nA failed verification step must fail visibly rather than silently producing a broken release.\\r\\n7. Domain Model (High Level)\\r\\nMinimum persistent entities:\\r\\n\\r\\nUser\\r\\nAnalysisTarget\\r\\nIngestionRun\\r\\nReview\\r\\nRecommended responsibilities:\\r\\n\\r\\nUser\\r\\nRepresents the authenticated account that owns application data.\\r\\n\\r\\nAnalysisTarget\\r\\nRepresents the product, business, location, or other entity whose reviews are being analyzed.\\r\\n\\r\\nMinimum information includes:\\r\\n\\r\\nowner\\r\\ntarget/entity name\\r\\nsupported platform\\r\\nsource URL or equivalent source reference\\r\\nIngestionRun\\r\\nRepresents an attempt to collect or import review data for an AnalysisTarget.\\r\\n\\r\\nUseful information includes:\\r\\n\\r\\ntarget\\r\\nstart/completion timestamps\\r\\nstatus\\r\\nsuccess/failure counts\\r\\nsource or ingestion metadata\\r\\nReview\\r\\nRepresents a single ingested customer review.\\r\\n\\r\\nMinimum information includes:\\r\\n\\r\\ningestion/target relationship\\r\\nrating\\r\\nreview text\\r\\nsource metadata when available\\r\\nOptional persistent entities may include ChatSession and ChatMessage if the team chooses to persist conversation history.\\r\\n\\r\\nRequired Relationships\\r\\nUser 1:N AnalysisTarget\\r\\nAnalysisTarget 1:N IngestionRun\\r\\nAnalysisTarget 1:N Review, directly or through IngestionRun\\r\\nIngestionRun 1:N Review when the design preserves ingestion history\\r\\nOwnership is transitive: reviews and ingestion data are accessible only through an AnalysisTarget owned by the authenticated user.\\r\\n\\r\\n8. Sprint Themes and Delivery Order\\r\\nThemes:\\r\\n\\r\\nSprint 1: Authentication, Analysis Targets, and Ingestion Baseline\\r\\nmulti-user authentication\\r\\nownership/security foundation\\r\\ntarget creation\\r\\ninitial review ingestion\\r\\nCI and unit-test baseline\\r\\nSprint 2: Ingestion Intelligence and Guardrailed Q\u0026A\\r\\ningestion result summary\\r\\nreview-grounded conversational analysis\\r\\nscope guard enforcement\\r\\nintegration testing and quality ratchet\\r\\nSprint 3: Production Deployment and Engineering Hardening\\r\\ncloud/hosted deployment\\r\\nautomated CI/CD\\r\\npost-deployment verification\\r\\nsecurity and reliability hardening\\r\\nend-to-end smoke coverage\\r\\n9. Student Execution Guidance\\r\\nTreat each sprint story file as authoritative for that sprint.\\r\\nUse canonical business rule IDs in the sprint file when implementing and testing stories.\\r\\nKeep Jira tickets one-to-one with stories.\\r\\nProvide explicit test evidence for each completed story.\\r\\nDo not implement later-sprint features early unless explicitly approved.\\r\\nAI-assisted development tools are permitted and expected, but students remain responsible for understanding, reviewing, testing, and securing the code they submit.\\r\\nMaintain complete AI-assisted development transcripts in an /ai-transcripts directory when transcripts are available from the tools being used. Do not intentionally remove dead ends or failed approaches from those transcripts.\\r\\nDocument the team\u0027s chosen review platform and important implementation assumptions.\\r\\nThe core requirements are the baseline. Teams may extend the product after required behavior is working and tested.\\r\\nDo not substitute fake integrations, hard-coded AI responses, or fabricated review data for required end-to-end behavior.\\r\\nUse professional judgment for implementation details that are not explicitly specified.\\r\\n10. Definition of Done (Project Level)\\r\\nA team is done when:\\r\\n\\r\\nA user can register, login, logout, and securely access the application.\\r\\nMultiple users can use the same deployed application with strict per-user data isolation.\\r\\nA user can create an analysis target and ingest or practically import real review data from the team\u0027s supported public review platform.\\r\\nThe application provides a trustworthy ingestion result summary.\\r\\nThe user can ask questions that are answered from the active ingested reviews.\\r\\nThe Q\u0026A system explicitly declines questions outside the active review scope.\\r\\nAuthentication, authorization, ingestion, summary, Q\u0026A, and scope-guard behaviors are test-backed.\\r\\nCI quality gates pass for the relevant sprint.\\r\\nThe application is deployed at a publicly reachable URL, with protected functionality requiring authentication.\\r\\nDeployment is performed through the team\u0027s CI/CD process and verified with post-deployment checks.\\r\\nThe complete source code is maintained in the team\u0027s GitHub repository.\\r\\nRequired project documentation and AI development transcripts are present and reviewable.\\r\\nThe deployed application is stable enough for an end-to-end class demonstration.\\r\\nReviewLens AI - Technical Specifications\\r\\nThis document defines implementation standards. It does not prescribe a single technology stack or application architecture unless a requirement explicitly says otherwise.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Scope and Relationship to Other Specifications\\r\\nUse project materials in this order:\\r\\n\\r\\nActive sprint requirements define required sprint scope and grading expectations.\\r\\nThe Product Requirements Document defines overall product scope and cross-sprint direction.\\r\\nUX specifications define user-experience direction.\\r\\nThese technical specifications define implementation standards and engineering guardrails.\\r\\nIf there is a conflict, follow the active sprint requirements for implementation scope and grading, then the Product Requirements Document for product intent.\\r\\n\\r\\nThese technical standards apply equally to human-written code, AI-generated code, AI-modified code, configuration, infrastructure, and test code.\\r\\n\\r\\nAI-generated implementation is not exempt from architecture, security, testing, or quality requirements.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. Architecture and Service Model\\r\\nBefore implementation begins, each team must establish a coherent architecture baseline.\\r\\n\\r\\n2.1 Sprint 1 Architecture Baseline\\r\\nEach team must prepare a short architecture baseline before substantial feature development begins.\\r\\n\\r\\nAt minimum, identify:\\r\\n\\r\\nFrontend technology.\\r\\nBackend technology.\\r\\nDatabase or persistence technology.\\r\\nManaged identity provider.\\r\\nReview-ingestion approach.\\r\\nLLM provider.\\r\\nDeployment platform.\\r\\nCore domain entities and conceptual relationships.\\r\\nMain API or service boundaries.\\r\\nMajor frontend screens or operating surfaces.\\r\\nSimple architecture diagram showing major application components and external services.\\r\\nThe baseline does not need to predict every later design decision. Teams may revise the architecture as they learn, but significant deviations should be intentional and explainable.\\r\\n\\r\\n2.2 Reference Architecture Expectations\\r\\nReviewLens is a multi-user SaaS web application.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe architecture must provide:\\r\\n\\r\\nA frontend application.\\r\\nA backend application or service layer.\\r\\nPersistent storage for user-owned application data.\\r\\nManaged user authenâ€¦4628 tokens truncatedâ€¦ck consistency. It must follow existing patterns, naming, and error handling.\\r\\n- Check security basics. Look for missing auth checks, missing ownership checks, unvalidated input, and secrets in code.\\r\\n- Check for hallucination. Confirm that functions, methods, options, and imports actually exist.\\r\\n- At least one teammate other than the author reviews every pull request. The author does not approve their own PR.\\r\\n- The PR description states whether AI assistance was used and which tool.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Validating generated dependencies and APIs\\r\\n\\r\\nAI tools can invent packages, functions, and endpoints, or suggest outdated or unsafe ones.\\r\\n\\r\\n- Verify every new package exists on the official registry, matches the intended name (watch for look-alike or typosquatted names), and is actively maintained.\\r\\n- Check the package\u0027s license, weekly downloads, and last release before adding it.\\r\\n- Add a dependency only when it is needed. Prefer what the stack already provides.\\r\\n- Verify every API call, method, or option against the official documentation (Next.js, Supabase, OpenAI), not just the AI\u0027s explanation.\\r\\n- Run `npm audit` after adding or updating dependencies and resolve high and critical findings.\\r\\n- Commit the lockfile with the change, and pin or review version ranges.\\r\\n- Never run install scripts or commands from AI output without reading them first.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Testing required before generated code is accepted\\r\\n\\r\\nGenerated code is accepted only when all of the following pass:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` pass with no new errors.\\r\\n- `npm run format:check` and `npm run lint` pass.\\r\\n- `npm test` passes, and unit tests cover the new logic, including invalid input and failure cases.\\r\\n- Tests exist for the security rules the change touches:\\r\\n  - **S1-BR-004:** a protected endpoint called without a valid session is rejected.\\r\\n  - **S1-BR-008:** a user cannot read, write, update, or ingest data belonging to another user.\\r\\n  - **S1-BR-011:** no secrets or provider credentials appear in source code or browser-delivered bundles.\\r\\n- The author manually exercises the feature (happy path and at least one failure path).\\r\\n- The GitHub Actions check \\\"Lint, typecheck, test, build\\\" (S1-007) passes on the pull request before merge. If CI is unavailable, run the same commands locally and paste the results into the PR.\\r\\n- Tests are written or reviewed by a human. A test that the AI wrote must be read to confirm it actually asserts something meaningful.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. Retaining AI session transcripts\\r\\n\\r\\nThe team keeps the complete available transcript of every AI session that produced or shaped committed code. A summary does not replace the original conversation.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Where:** `ai-transcripts/` at the root of the repository.\\r\\n- **Naming:** `YYYY-MM-DD_\u003cauthor\u003e_\u003cissue-id\u003e_\u003ctool\u003e.md` (for example `2026-11-02_shahdp2_S1-007_claude.md`).\\r\\n- **What to save:** the original prompts and AI responses, including failed approaches, errors, corrections, and dead ends, plus a short note on what was accepted, changed, or rejected.\\r\\n- **Redact first:** remove secrets, credentials, and personal data before committing a transcript.\\r\\n- **Assume visibility:** transcripts are committed to the repository, so assume they may be seen by the whole team and the instructor.\\r\\n- **Link it:** reference the transcript path in the pull request description.\\r\\n- **Retain** transcripts for the duration of the course project. They are part of the project record and may be reviewed by the instructor.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. When generated code requires additional security review\\r\\n\\r\\nAny pull request that includes AI-generated code in the areas below needs a second reviewer who specifically checks security, in addition to the normal review:\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Authentication and sessions.** Any protected backend route must verify the authenticated identity on the server. Hiding frontend controls is not access protection (S1-BR-004).\\r\\n- **Authorization and ownership.** Any code that reads, writes, updates, or deletes user-owned data must enforce ownership server-side, and cross-user operations must be denied (S1-BR-008). Row Level Security must be enabled on all tables holding user data, and its policies must restrict access to the owning user.\\r\\n- **Ingestion.** Any code that parses uploaded CSV/JSON or writes review data, including validation, size limits, and ownership of the target.\\r\\n- **Database access.** SQL, migrations, Row Level Security policies, and any use of a privileged or service-role key.\\r\\n- **Secrets and configuration.** Environment variables, provider credentials, database credentials, review-service credentials, and anything that could end up in client-side code. `.env` files must be in `.gitignore`. Only values a provider explicitly designs to be public (such as the Supabase anon key, exposed through `NEXT_PUBLIC_` variables) may reach the browser. The Supabase service-role key and the OpenAI key stay server-side only (S1-BR-011).\\r\\n- **Third-party calls.** Code that calls the OpenAI API or another external service, especially what data is sent.\\r\\n- **New or upgraded dependencies** with network, file-system, or install-script access.\\r\\n- **Error handling.** Responses must not leak stack traces, secret keys, or internal diagnostics to the browser.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Definition of Done (applies equally to AI-generated code)\\r\\n\\r\\n- [ ] Meets the story\u0027s acceptance criteria\\r\\n- [ ] Reviewed and approved by a teammate other than the author\\r\\n- [ ] `npm run format:check`, `npm run lint`, `npm run typecheck`, `npm test`, and `npm run build` pass, and the GitHub Actions check \\\"Lint, typecheck, test, build\\\" passes on the PR\\r\\n- [ ] Security rules S1-BR-004, S1-BR-008, and S1-BR-011 verified where applicable\\r\\n- [ ] Dependencies and APIs verified against official sources\\r\\n- [ ] No secrets in source control or client-delivered code\\r\\n- [ ] AI session transcript saved and linked in the PR (if AI was used)\\r\\n# ReviewLens AI Engineering Standards\\r\\n\\r\\n## 1. Purpose and Authority\\r\\n\\r\\nThis document defines the engineering standards that developers and AI coding tools must follow when contributing to ReviewLens AI. Its purpose is to keep the codebase consistent, secure, maintainable, and aligned with the team\u0027s architecture and active sprint requirements.\\r\\n\\r\\nThese standards apply to source code, configuration, automated tests, pull requests, and AI-generated changes. If this document conflicts with an active sprint requirement, the active sprint requirement takes priority and this document must be updated through a reviewed pull request.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is a living team document. Proposed changes must be discussed in the related pull request and approved before the standard is changed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Approved Technology Baseline\\r\\n\\r\\n### Frontend\\r\\n\\r\\n- Next.js App Router\\r\\n- React\\r\\n- TypeScript in strict mode\\r\\n- Tailwind CSS\\r\\n- shadcn/ui\\r\\n\\r\\n### Backend\\r\\n\\r\\n- Next.js Route Handlers\\r\\n- Server-side TypeScript services\\r\\n\\r\\n### Database and Authentication\\r\\n\\r\\n- Supabase PostgreSQL\\r\\n- Supabase Auth\\r\\n- Supabase Row Level Security (RLS) as an additional database-level authorization layer\\r\\n\\r\\n### Testing\\r\\n\\r\\n- Jest\\r\\n- React Testing Library\\r\\n- Playwright\\r\\n\\r\\n### Delivery and External Services\\r\\n\\r\\n- npm as the only package manager\\r\\n- GitHub Actions for continuous integration\\r\\n- Vercel for application deployment\\r\\n- Supabase hosted services for authentication and persistence\\r\\n- OpenAI API for later review-analysis functionality\\r\\n- Review provider/API pending final team and professor approval\\r\\n\\r\\nThe exact supported versions and generated configuration are established by S1-005 and S1-006 and must be recorded in the repository lockfile and setup documentation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 3. Repository Organization\\r\\n\\r\\nThe following structure is the team\u0027s proposed baseline. S1-005 is responsible for creating and validating the actual Next.js structure. If the generated framework structure requires a change, S1-005 must update this document in the same pull request or in a linked standards pull request.\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nsrc/\\r\\n|-- app/\\r\\n|   |-- login/\\r\\n|   |-- analysis-targets/\\r\\n|   `-- api/\\r\\n|-- components/\\r\\n|   |-- ui/\\r\\n|   |-- analysis-targets/\\r\\n|   |-- ingestion/\\r\\n|   `-- shared/\\r\\n|-- services/\\r\\n|   |-- auth/\\r\\n|   |-- analysis-targets/\\r\\n|   |-- ingestion/\\r\\n|   `-- reviews/\\r\\n|-- lib/\\r\\n|   |-- supabase/\\r\\n|   |-- validation/\\r\\n|   `-- utils/\\r\\n|-- types/\\r\\n`-- proxy.ts\\r\\n\\r\\ntests/\\r\\n|-- unit/\\r\\n|-- integration/\\r\\n`-- e2e/\\r\\n\\r\\ndocs/\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nRepository rules:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Routes, layouts, pages, and Route Handlers belong under `src/app`.\\r\\n- Reusable UI belongs under `src/components`; shadcn/ui primitives belong under `src/components/ui`.\\r\\n- Business and data-access logic belongs in server-side services rather than directly in React components.\\r\\n- Route Handlers should remain thin: validate the request, authenticate the user, authorize the resource, call a service, and return a response.\\r\\n- Shared framework clients and utilities belong under `src/lib`.\\r\\n- Shared TypeScript types belong under `src/types` when they are not better located beside a single feature.\\r\\n- Unit and integration tests should mirror the application area they test. End-to-end tests belong under `tests/e2e`.\\r\\n- `public`, `package.json`, lockfiles, environment templates, and framework configuration remain at the repository root as required by Next.js.\\r\\n- With Next.js 16, request proxy logic uses `src/proxy.ts`. Authentication and authorization must not rely on Proxy alone; protected server operations must verify access independently.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Naming Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- React component names and component files use PascalCase, for example `TargetCard.tsx`.\\r\\n- Functions, variables, and object properties use camelCase.\\r\\n- Custom React hooks begin with `use`, for example `useAnalysisTargets.ts`.\\r\\n- Constants that are truly fixed application-wide values use UPPER_SNAKE_CASE.\\r\\n- Non-component TypeScript files and feature folders use lowercase kebab-case, for example `review-parser.ts` and `analysis-targets`.\\r\\n- Test files use `.test.ts` or `.test.tsx`. Playwright files use `.spec.ts`.\\r\\n- Next.js special files keep their required framework names, including `page.tsx`, `layout.tsx`, `loading.tsx`, `error.tsx`, and `route.ts`.\\r\\n- PostgreSQL tables and columns use snake_case.\\r\\n- Domain terminology must be consistent: use `AnalysisTarget`, `Review`, and `IngestionAttempt` in TypeScript and the corresponding snake_case names in the database.\\r\\n\\r\\nNames should describe intent. Avoid unclear abbreviations and generic names such as `data`, `thing`, or `helper` when a more specific name is available.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Formatting, Static Analysis, and Commands\\r\\n\\r\\nRequired tools:\\r\\n\\r\\n- ESLint\\r\\n- Prettier\\r\\n- TypeScript strict mode\\r\\n\\r\\nFormatting rules:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Use two-space indentation.\\r\\n- Remove unused imports and variables.\\r\\n- Do not commit commented-out code or temporary debugging statements.\\r\\n- Prefer clear, typed code over unnecessary abstraction.\\r\\n- Do not suppress a TypeScript or ESLint error without explaining why in code and in the pull request.\\r\\n\\r\\nS1-006 must configure these commands in `package.json`:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `npm run lint`\\r\\n- `npm run format:check`\\r\\n- `npm run test`\\r\\n- `npm run build`\\r\\n\\r\\nAll applicable commands must pass before a code pull request is merged. Until S1-006 establishes the scripts, contributors must not claim that an unconfigured check has passed.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. Error-Handling Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\n- Validate external input at an authoritative server boundary even when the browser also validates it.\\r\\n- Handle expected errors explicitly and translate them into safe, understandable responses.\\r\\n- Do not expose stack traces, SQL details, internal file paths, secrets, credentials, or provider responses containing sensitive information.\\r\\n- Unexpected errors must be logged safely on the server and returned to the user as a generic error.\\r\\n- Existing valid review data must not be replaced by fabricated or placeholder data when ingestion fails.\\r\\n\\r\\nStandard HTTP status usage:\\r\\n\\r\\n- `200` - successful request\\r\\n- `201` - resource created\\r\\n- `400` - invalid request or validation failure\\r\\n- `401` - authentication required or invalid session\\r\\n- `403` - authenticated user is not permitted to perform the operation\\r\\n- `404` - requested resource is unavailable or cannot be disclosed\\r\\n- `409` - conflict, such as an operation that violates a uniqueness rule\\r\\n- `500` - unexpected server failure\\r\\n\\r\\nThe team must apply a consistent `403`/`404` policy to user-owned resources so responses do not reveal another user\u0027s private resource. The selected policy must be tested.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. API and Service Response Conventions\\r\\n\\r\\nSuccessful Route Handler response:\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\\"success\\\": true,\\r\\n  \\\"data\\\": {}\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nError response:\\r\\n\\r\\n```json\\r\\n{\\r\\n  \\\"success\\\": false,\\r\\n  \\\"error\\\": {\\r\\n    \\\"code\\\": \\\"INVALID_SOURCE_URL\\\",\\r\\n    \\\"message\\\": \\\"The submitted source URL is invalid.\\\"\\r\\n  }\\r\\n}\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nRules:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Error `code` values are stable machine-readable identifiers; `message` values are safe user-readable explanations.\\r\\n- Validation responses may include safe field-level details when useful.\\r\\n- Route Handlers must use the shared response convention instead of inventing a new shape per endpoint.\\r\\n- Service functions return typed results or throw documented application errors; they do not return ad hoc response objects.\\r\\n- Protected endpoints derive identity from the verified Supabase session, never from a client-supplied owner ID.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 8. Authentication, Authorization, and Ownership\\r\\n\\r\\n- Every AnalysisTarget belongs to exactly one authenticated user.\\r\\n- Review and IngestionAttempt access is authorized through the owning AnalysisTarget.\\r\\n- Protected Route Handlers must verify the session, derive authenticated identity, verify resource ownership, and only then perform the operation.\\r\\n- The frontend may improve navigation by hiding unavailable actions, but frontend filtering is never an authorization control.\\r\\n- Client-supplied `userId`, `ownerId`, or equivalent values are not trusted for ownership decisions.\\r\\n- Supabase RLS provides defense in depth but does not replace authorization inside protected server operations.\\r\\n- Cross-user tests must use at least two identities and prove that User B cannot read, modify, delete, or ingest into User A\u0027s resources.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 9. Logging Expectations\\r\\n\\r\\nLog server-side events that help diagnose or audit the system, including:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Authentication and authorization failures\\r\\n- Ingestion start, success, partial success, and failure\\r\\n- External-provider failures\\r\\n- Database failures\\r\\n- Unexpected application failures\\r\\n\\r\\nLogs should include safe context such as operation name, error category, and a request or correlation identifier when available.\\r\\n\\r\\nNever log:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Passwords\\r\\n- Session cookies, authentication tokens, or authorization headers\\r\\n- API keys, database credentials, or complete environment values\\r\\n- Supabase service-role keys\\r\\n- Unredacted personal information\\r\\n- Full customer-review text in routine production logs\\r\\n\\r\\nUse the shared project logger once it is established. Do not leave temporary `console.log` debugging statements in merged code.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 10. Adding and Updating Dependencies\\r\\n\\r\\nThe team uses npm and commits `package-lock.json`. Do not use Yarn or pnpm in this repository.\\r\\n\\r\\nBefore adding a dependency:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Confirm that it solves a specific project need.\\r\\n2. Confirm that an existing dependency or platform capability does not already solve the problem.\\r\\n3. Verify that the package is actively maintained and compatible with the project\u0027s Node.js and Next.js versions.\\r\\n4. Review its license and known security concerns.\\r\\n5. Place it in `dependencies` or `devDependencies` correctly.\\r\\n6. Explain the addition in the pull request.\\r\\n\\r\\nInstall packages through npm rather than manually editing dependency versions. Unrelated dependency upgrades must not be included in a feature pull request.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 11. Testing Standards\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery code change that adds or modifies behavior must include appropriate automated tests. Documentation-only changes should state that automated tests are not applicable and identify the review performed instead.\\r\\n\\r\\nDepending on the behavior, tests should cover:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Successful behavior\\r\\n- Expected failure behavior\\r\\n- Validation boundaries\\r\\n- Authenticated and unauthenticated requests\\r\\n- Authorized and unauthorized users\\r\\n- Persistence and ownership where applicable\\r\\n- Loading and error states for interactive UI\\r\\n\\r\\nExternal review-provider APIs and OpenAI calls must be mocked or replaced with deterministic fixtures in CI. Automated tests must not depend entirely on a live external website or paid provider.\\r\\n\\r\\nSecurity-sensitive features require explicit User A/User B isolation tests. A test must demonstrate denial at the backend or database boundary, not merely that the other user\u0027s record is hidden in the UI.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 12. AI-Assisted Development\\r\\n\\r\\nAI-generated code is treated as team-authored code and must meet the same standards and Definition of Done.\\r\\n\\r\\nDevelopers must:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Provide the relevant story, architecture, standards, and existing code as prompt context.\\r\\n- Review and understand generated changes before accepting them.\\r\\n- Verify generated APIs, packages, and configuration against authoritative documentation.\\r\\n- Test generated behavior, including failure and security cases.\\r\\n- Retain the required AI-session transcript according to the convention established by S1-004.\\r\\n- Request additional human review for authentication, authorization, RLS, secret handling, ingestion, and AI-data-boundary changes.\\r\\n\\r\\nAI output is a proposal, not proof that a change is correct.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 13. Pull Request Requirements\\r\\n\\r\\nEvery pull request must:\\r\\n\\r\\n- Link the relevant GitHub issue.\\r\\n- Describe what changed and why.\\r\\n- Stay within the assigned story\u0027s scope.\\r\\n- Identify tests or review evidence.\\r\\n- Pass all applicable build, formatting, lint, and automated-test checks.\\r\\n- Contain no real secrets or unrelated generated files.\\r\\n- Receive approval from at least one teammate before merge.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe author must respond to review feedback. A pull request is not Done until it is approved, required checks pass, it is merged into `main`, and the associated board item is updated.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 14. Environment and Secret Requirements\\r\\n\\r\\n- Real secrets must never be committed.\\r\\n- Local secrets belong in `.env.local`, which must be ignored by Git.\\r\\n- `.env.example` documents required variable names using empty or clearly fake placeholder values.\\r\\n- Variables beginning with `NEXT_PUBLIC_` are exposed to the browser and must never contain private credentials.\\r\\n- Supabase service-role keys, database credentials, OpenAI keys, and private provider credentials are server-only.\\r\\n- Environment-specific values must not be hard-coded into source files.\\r\\n- Missing required configuration must produce a clear startup or runtime error without exposing the secret value.\\r\\n- If a secret is accidentally committed, stop using it, notify the team, rotate or revoke it immediately, and follow the team\u0027s incident procedure. Deleting it from the latest file alone is not sufficient.\\r\\n\\r\\nS1-008 owns the final `.env.example`, ignore rules, environment-validation approach, and setup instructions. When S1-008 changes these conventions, this document must remain consistent with that implementation.\\r\\n\"}"
                   }
               ],

authenticates.\\r\\nThe user creates an analysis target.\\r\\nThe user submits a valid review source.\\r\\nReview data is ingested and persisted.\\r\\nThe persisted reviews remain associated with the correct analysis target and authenticated user.\\r\\nA second authenticated user cannot access the first user\u0027s targets or review data.\\r\\nThe team should demonstrate the workflow as a coherent application experience rather than presenting each Jira story independently.\\r\\n\\r\\nCI/CD and Testing Requirements (Required)\\r\\nThese requirements apply to all Sprint 1 stories.\\r\\n\\r\\nCI/CD Baseline Gate\\r\\nEvery pull request must automatically run:\\r\\nlint or equivalent static checks\\r\\napplication build\\r\\nautomated unit tests\\r\\nA pull request with any required failing check cannot be merged through the team\u0027s normal protected-branch workflow.\\r\\nCI output must be visible to reviewers before merge.\\r\\nRequired checks must run from repository configuration rather than relying on a developer to execute them manually.\\r\\nApplication secrets, credentials, and API keys must not be committed to source control.\\r\\nTest Baseline Expectations\\r\\nEvery feature story must include at least one automated test update.\\r\\nTests should verify observable behavior rather than implementation details whenever practical.\\r\\nAuthentication and authorization stories must include negative-path tests.\\r\\nValidation stories must test both accepted and rejected input.\\r\\nPersistence stories must verify that created data can be retrieved from the persistence layer.\\r\\nOwnership tests must demonstrate that cross-user access is denied.\\r\\nIngestion parsing or normalization should be tested using deterministic sample data or fixtures.\\r\\nCI tests should not depend on the continued availability or exact content of a live third-party review website.\\r\\nExternal dependencies should be mocked, stubbed, recorded, or otherwise isolated in automated tests where practical.\\r\\nMinimum Test Evidence by Story Type\\r\\nUser-Facing Behavior\\r\\nHappy-path behavior.\\r\\nAt least one non-happy-path behavior.\\r\\nValidation\\r\\nValid input succeeds.\\r\\nInvalid input fails with an appropriate application response.\\r\\nPersistence\\r\\nData is created or updated.\\r\\nPersisted data can be retrieved.\\r\\nOwnership relationships are preserved.\\r\\nAuthentication and Authorization\\r\\nAuthorized request succeeds.\\r\\nUnauthenticated request is denied where authentication is required.\\r\\nCross-user request is denied where ownership applies.\\r\\nIngestion\\r\\nRepresentative valid input is processed.\\r\\nMalformed or unsupported input is handled safely.\\r\\nExpected review fields are normalized correctly.\\r\\nFailure does not silently create fabricated review data.\\r\\nDefinition of Done Additions\\r\\nA Sprint 1 story is not complete until:\\r\\n\\r\\nThe story outcome works in the running application.\\r\\nRequired automated tests pass locally.\\r\\nRequired automated tests pass in CI.\\r\\nThe story does not knowingly break previously completed behavior.\\r\\nThe pull request contains a short Test Evidence section describing the tests used to verify the story.\\r\\nSignificant technical assumptions or limitations are documented where another developer would reasonably need to know about them.\\r\\nCanonical Business Rules (Sprint 1)\\r\\nThese rules are authoritative for Sprint 1 implementation and grading.\\r\\n\\r\\nS1-BR Authentication and Identity\\r\\nS1-BR-001: Authentication must use a managed identity service such as Clerk, Supabase Auth, Auth0, Firebase Authentication, Amazon Cognito, or an equivalent service approved by the instructor.\\r\\nS1-BR-002: Teams are not expected to implement their own password storage or credential authentication system.\\r\\nS1-BR-003: Authentication is required for all protected ReviewLens application functionality.\\r\\nS1-BR-004: Protected backend requests must verify authenticated identity. Hiding frontend controls is not sufficient access protection.\\r\\nS1-BR-005: The authenticated identity supplied by the identity provider must be mapped to the application\u0027s user-owned data.\\r\\nS1-BR-006: After logout, the previous authenticated application session must no longer provide access to protected application functionality.\\r\\nS1-BR Data Ownership and Security\\r\\nS1-BR-007: All user-scoped ReviewLens records must be associated with an authenticated owner.\\r\\nS1-BR-008: Cross-user reads, writes, updates, and ingestion operations must be denied.\\r\\nS1-BR-009: Ownership authorization must be enforced on the backend or persistence security layer. Frontend filtering is not sufficient.\\r\\nS1-BR-010: A client-supplied user ID or owner ID must not by itself be trusted as proof of ownership.\\r\\nS1-BR-011: Application secrets, provider credentials, database credentials, and review-service credentials must not be exposed in source control or browser-delivered code unless the credential is explicitly designed by the provider to be public.\\r\\nS1-BR Analysis Targets\\r\\nS1-BR-012: Each team supports one public review platform for the capsâ€¦1113 tokens truncatedâ€¦ider handles registration, credentials, sessions, and password reset. It does not handle authorization.\\r\\nReviewLens AI - UX and Interface Guidance\\r\\nThis is not a component checklist and it is not a design template. Teams may choose their own layout, visual style, and interaction patterns, but those decisions must produce one coherent product from beginning to end.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. How To Use These UX Specifications\\r\\nUse project materials in this order:\\r\\n\\r\\nActive sprint requirements define required sprint scope and grading expectations.\\r\\nThe Product Requirements Document defines overall product direction.\\r\\nThese UX specifications define user-experience standards and consistency expectations.\\r\\nThe technical specifications define implementation standards and engineering guardrails.\\r\\nIf there is a conflict, follow the active sprint requirements for implementation scope.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe most important idea is simple:\\r\\n\\r\\nThe application should feel like one product, not three sprint submissions stitched together.\\r\\n\\r\\nUsers should not feel that they are moving between unrelated screens, disconnected workflows, or inconsistent interaction patterns.\\r\\n\\r\\n2. Product Experience Direction\\r\\nReviewLens is an analysis-centered application.\\r\\n\\r\\nEverything in the interface should help a user move from identifying what they want to analyze to understanding the available review evidence.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe primary product flow is:\\r\\n\\r\\nAnalysisTarget Ã¢â€ â€™ Ingestion Ã¢â€ â€™ Dataset Summary Ã¢â€ â€™ Review Q\u0026A\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a user enters the application, they should be able to understand:\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat entities they are analyzing.\\r\\nWhich AnalysisTarget is active.\\r\\nWhich review platform supplied the active data.\\r\\nWhether usable review data exists.\\r\\nWhat the ingestion result means.\\r\\nWhat dataset the summary represents.\\r\\nWhat dataset ReviewLens is using for Q\u0026A.\\r\\nThe application should reduce ambiguity around the analysis context.\\r\\n\\r\\nReviewLens should feel like a focused review-intelligence product, not a general-purpose chatbot.\\r\\n\\r\\n3. Navigation Expectations\\r\\nEach team may choose its navigation pattern.\\r\\n\\r\\nAcceptable approaches include:\\r\\n\\r\\nTop navigation.\\r\\nLeft navigation.\\r\\nHybrid navigation.\\r\\nAnother coherent design.\\r\\nWhat is not acceptable is changing navigation paradigms arbitrarily across the application.\\r\\n\\r\\nIf the application begins with one approach, major screens and workflows should follow that approach consistently.\\r\\n\\r\\nNavigation should make it easy to:\\r\\n\\r\\nFind existing AnalysisTargets.\\r\\nCreate a new AnalysisTarget.\\r\\nOpen the active AnalysisTarget workspace.\\r\\nMove between major product areas without losing context.\\r\\nAccess account or settings functionality when applicable.\\r\\nNot every final destination must appear in every sprint. Follow the active sprint requirements for required functionality.\\r\\n\\r\\nMoving between major application areas should not cause the user to lose track of the active AnalysisTarget.\\r\\n\\r\\n4. AnalysisTarget Workspace\\r\\nThe AnalysisTarget workspace is the primary operating surface for ReviewLens and should receive the most design attention.\\r\\n\\r\\nThis is where the user should be able to understand and work with the active analysis context.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe workspace should make clear:\\r\\n\\r\\nTarget or entity name.\\r\\nReview platform.\\r\\nSource or source reference.\\r\\nIngestion state.\\r\\nCurrent review dataset.\\r\\nIngestion summary.\\r\\nAvailable Q\u0026A functionality.\\r\\nTeams may choose how this workspace is organized.\\r\\n\\r\\nPossible approaches include:\\r\\n\\r\\nTabs.\\r\\nPanels.\\r\\nCards.\\r\\nSections.\\r\\nSplit views.\\r\\nAnother coherent model.\\r\\nThe important requirement is that ingestion, summary, and Q\u0026A feel like parts of one analysis workflow rather than unrelated application screens.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe user should not need to infer which AnalysisTarget or review dataset is currently being analyzed.\\r\\n\\r\\n5. AnalysisTarget Management Experience\\r\\nCreating, reopening, switching, and managing AnalysisTargets should be straightforward.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe interface should make it easy to:\\r\\n\\r\\nCreate a new AnalysisTarget.\\r\\nIdentify the entity being analyzed.\\r\\nIdentify the supported review platform.\\r\\nIdentify the review source.\\r\\nReopen an existing target.\\r\\nSwitch between owned targets.\\r\\nUnderstand which target is active.\\r\\nDelete an owned target when that functionality is available.\\r\\nTarget creation should use clear validation and meaningful feedback.\\r\\n\\r\\nInvalid input should not create confusing partial state.\\r\\n\\r\\nSwitching AnalysisTargets should produce a visible and understandable change in the active application context.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe user should never have to guess whether they are still viewing or analyzing the prior target.\\r\\n\\r\\n6. Review Ingestion Experience\\r\\nReview ingestion should not feel like a black box.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen ingestion begins, the user should understand:\\r\\n\\r\\nWhat source is being processed.\\r\\nWhich AnalysisTarget will receive the resulting review data.\\r\\nWhether processing is underway.\\r\\nWhether ingestion succeeds.\\r\\nWhether ingestion fails.\\r\\nWhether ingestion partially succeeds, when supported.\\r\\nWhether records were skipped or rejected.\\r\\nWhether the resulting dataset is ready for analysis.\\r\\nThe application should clearly distinguish important states such as:\\r\\n\\r\\nNo ingestion has occurred.\\r\\nIngestion is processing.\\r\\nIngestion succeeded.\\r\\nIngestion failed.\\r\\nIngestion partially succeeded.\\r\\nThe user should not click an ingestion action and then be left wondering whether data was collected or whether ReviewLens is ready to answer questions.\\r\\n\\r\\n6.1 Ingestion Result Summary\\r\\nThe ingestion summary should communicate enough information to give the user confidence in the active dataset.\\r\\n\\r\\nAt minimum, the user should be able to understand:\\r\\n\\r\\nWhich AnalysisTarget the summary represents.\\r\\nHow many reviews were successfully ingested.\\r\\nBasic rating information.\\r\\nWhether records were rejected or skipped when applicable.\\r\\nWhether the active dataset is usable for Q\u0026A.\\r\\nSummary information should be easy to scan.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe UX should not force the user to inspect raw review rows merely to determine whether ingestion worked.\\r\\n\\r\\n7. Review Intelligence and Q\u0026A Experience\\r\\nReviewLens Q\u0026A should feel like analysis of the active review dataset, not open-ended AI chat.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe interface should maintain visible context around:\\r\\n\\r\\nThe active AnalysisTarget.\\r\\nThe review platform.\\r\\nThe active dataset.\\r\\nWhether review data is available.\\r\\nWhether the system is ready to answer review-grounded questions.\\r\\nThe Q\u0026A interaction should be simple and focused.\\r\\n\\r\\nAt minimum, it should support:\\r\\n\\r\\nNatural-language question input.\\r\\nClear submit action.\\r\\nProcessing or loading state.\\r\\nResponse presentation.\\r\\nMeaningful failure state.\\r\\nPersistent multi-session chat history is not required unless active sprint requirements say otherwise.\\r\\n\\r\\n7.1 Distinct Q\u0026A Outcomes\\r\\nThe interface should distinguish among four fundamentally different outcomes:\\r\\n\\r\\nGrounded answer\\r\\nReviewLens can answer from the active reviews.\\r\\n\\r\\nInsufficient evidence\\r\\nThe question is relevant, but the available reviews do not support a reliable answer.\\r\\n\\r\\nOut-of-scope refusal\\r\\n\"}"
                   }

Please sign in again.\\\" |\\r\\n| Authorization denial           | Full-page error state in the content area          | \\\"This target isn\u0027t available to your account. Check that the link opens a Google Maps page.\\\"                      | Check the link, or create a new target with a different link |\\r\\n| Source returned no usable reviews | \\\"No usable reviews were found for this source, so nothing was saved.\\\"                                       | Create a new target with a different link                    |\\r\\n| Provider times out                | \\\"The review service took too long to respond. Please try again.\\\"                                            | Retry                                                        |\\r\\n| Provider quota exhausted          | \\\"Review collection is temporarily unavailable because our usage limit was reached. Please try again later.\\\" | Try again later                                              |\\r\\n| Provider unreachable              | \\\"We couldn\u0027t reach the review service. Please try again later.\\\"                                             | Retry                                                        |\\r\\n\\r\\n4.5 Error text rules:  \\r\\nPlain language, three sentences max, and a next step if applicable. Never display stack traces, database messages, HTTP client exceptions, provider SDK or API error text, internal IDs, or secrets. Technical detail only goes to server logs. A failed ingestion never shows reviews that were not actually collected and previously saved reviews remain visible and unchanged.  \\r\\nErrors that appear after an action use role=\\\"alert\\\" so screen readers announce them. Errors stay in place until the user retries the action or leaves the page; do not auto dismiss errors.\\r\\n\\r\\n**5\\\\. Loading and processing states**\\r\\n\\r\\n1. **Page/data loading:** Use shadcn/ui Skeleton placeholders that match the final layout (title bar plus card or grid shapes). Do not use a bare full-page spinner for page loads.\\r\\n2. **Button/action in progress:** The button is disabled, its label changes to the progressive form (\\\"CreatingÃ¢â‚¬Â¦\\\", \\\"Starting ingestion...\\\"), and a small spinner appears before the label. Keep the button width stable.\\r\\n3. **Spinner component:** One shared spinner component. No one builds a second loading indicator\\r\\n4. **Never leave the user wondering:** Any action that takes more than 500 ms shows a visible processing state and the UI never shows stale success while a new request is running.\\r\\n5. **Ingestion status model:** IngestionStatus is the single component that renders Ingestion state, using the same badge everywhere (target card, workspace header, ingestion panel):\\r\\n\\r\\n| State                          | Badge label        | Color        | Panel behavior                                                                                                                                  |\\r\\n| :----------------------------- | :----------------- | :----------- | :---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| No ingestion has occurred      | Not ingested       | Neutral gray | Shows \\\"Start ingestion\\\" as the primary action and a one-line explanation.                                                                       |\\r\\n| Processing                     | Ingesting          | Blue         | \\\"Start ingestion\\\" is disabled; text states which target and source are being processed; status refreshes automatically until it ends.           |\\r\\n| Succeeded                      | Ingested           | Green        | Shows the number of reviews saved; ReviewList shows the persisted reviews                                                                       |\\r\\n| Succeeded with skipped records | Partially ingested | Amber        | Shows saved and skipped counts, explains that only saved reviews enter the dataset, and retains the existing completed-run action restrictions. |\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\"}"
                   }
               ],
    "internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":  {

Mechanism\\r\\n\\r\\n| Piece         | What it is                                                                                                                                                                   |\\r\\n| ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |\\r\\n| Coverage data | Jest\u0027s V8 provider (`coverageProvider: \\\"v8\\\"` in `jest.config.ts`), written as `coverage/coverage-final.json`                                                                 |\\r\\n| Changed lines | `git diff --unified=0 --find-renames` from the merge-base of the base branch and `HEAD`, plus uncommitted and untracked files                                                |\\r\\n| Gate          | `scripts/coverage/changed-coverage.ts` (logic, unit-tested) run by `scripts/coverage/check-changed-coverage.ts` (entry point) with Node\u0027s built-in TypeScript type stripping |\\r\\n| Policy        | `scripts/coverage/changed-coverage-policy.ts`                                                                                                                                |\\r\\n| CI            | `.github/workflows/ci.yml`: `Unit tests (with coverage)` then `Changed-code coverage` (pull requests only)                                                                   |\\r\\n\\r\\nNo new dependency, no external service, no secrets and no network access. The diff runs entirely\\r\\nagainst the local clone, so CI checks out with `fetch-depth: 0`.\\r\\n\\r\\nA line is **coverable** when the coverage report lists a statement starting on it, and\\r\\n**covered** when that statement ran at least once (Istanbul line semantics). Branch coverage of\\r\\nchanged lines is printed for information but does not gate: V8 branch ranges are too coarse to\\r\\nattribute reliably to individual changed lines.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 2. Policy\\r\\n\\r\\nA pull request passes only when **all** of these hold:\\r\\n\\r\\n1. At least **80%** of all coverable changed lines are covered.\\r\\n2. At least **90%** of coverable changed lines inside `src/services/qa/**` are covered. The Q\u0026A\\r\\n   orchestration layer must stay verified by deterministic tests (S2-BR-027 to S2-BR-031). Every\\r\\n   open Q\u0026A PR at the time of adoption (#78, #79, #84) measured 100% here.\\r\\n3. **No changed file has 0% changed-line coverage.** This stops one untested module from hiding\\r\\n   inside an otherwise well-tested PR.\\r\\n\\r\\nWhen a PR has no coverable changed lines (documentation, tests, CSS, SQL or workflow changes only),\\r\\nthe gate passes and says so.\\r\\n\\r\\n### Edge cases\\r\\n\\r\\n| Situation                                                   | Bâ€¦1072 tokens truncatedâ€¦------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |\\r\\n| `scripts/coverage/check-changed-coverage.ts` | Thin process entry point (argument/environment/output wiring only, no logic). Jest cannot load its top-level `await` and `import.meta`; its behavior is verified by a subprocess test in `tests/unit/scripts/coverage/changed-coverage.test.ts`, and all logic lives in the measured `changed-coverage.ts`. |\\r\\n\\r\\nExclusions are never applied to files containing real logic, and lowering a threshold is not an\\r\\nacceptable substitute for tests.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 4. Run it locally\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\ngit fetch origin main          # the gate compares against origin/main\\r\\nnpm run coverage:changed       # full Jest run with coverage, then the gate\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nOther forms:\\r\\n\\r\\n```bash\\r\\nnpm run coverage:changed:check                     # gate only; reuse coverage/coverage-final.json\\r\\nnpm run coverage:changed -- --base origin/develop  # compare against another base\\r\\nnpm run coverage:changed:check -- --coverage path/to/coverage-final.json\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\nThe local result includes uncommitted and untracked files, so it matches what the PR will show once\\r\\npushed. Always use a **full** coverage run: `jest \u003csingle file\u003e --coverage` produces a partial\\r\\nreport, and the gate will refuse it rather than guess. Requires Node 24 (the team baseline);\\r\\nNode 23.6 or later also works.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 5. Reading a failure\\r\\n\\r\\n```text\\r\\nChanged-code coverage (S2-001) vs origin/main (merge-base ed686a266df6)\\r\\nPolicy: at least 80% of coverable changed lines; 90% within src/services/qa/**; no changed file may have 0% changed-line coverage.\\r\\n\\r\\nFile                     Covered/Changed  Line %   Uncovered changed lines\\r\\nsrc/lib/rating-label.ts            10/19   52.63%  11-19\\r\\n\\r\\nOverall changed-line coverage: 52.63% (10/19); required 80%.\\r\\n\\r\\nFAIL: changed-code coverage requirements are not met:\\r\\n  - Changed-line coverage is 52.63% (10/19); the required minimum is 80%.\\r\\n```\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Uncovered changed lines** are line numbers in the file as it is in your branch. Open the file,\\r\\n  find those lines, and add a test that executes them, usually a failure, validation or edge case\\r\\n  that has no test yet.\\r\\n- **\\\"none of its N changed lines are executed\\\"** means no test imports the file at all.\\r\\n- In CI the same table appears in the job\u0027s **Summary** tab (`$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`), and the full\\r\\n  text is in the `Changed-code coverage` step log.\\r\\n- **Exit 2** means the check could not run (no coverage file, partial or corrupt report, base\\r\\n  branch not fetched). The message says what to run; this never counts as a pass.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 6. Requesting an exclusion\\r\\n\\r\\nExclusions are for code that cannot reasonably be unit-tested in Jest **and** has no real logic:\\r\\nfor example, a thin Next.js `loading.tsx` that is verified by Playwright.\\r\\n\\r\\n1. Add an entry to `exclusions` in `scripts/coverage/changed-coverage-policy.ts` with the\\r\\n   narrowest pattern possible (prefer one file over a glob) and a specific `reason`.\\r\\n2. Add the same row to the exclusions table in section 3.\\r\\n3. In the PR description, explain why a Jest test is not practical and which other test (for\\r\\n   example a Playwright spec) covers the behavior.\\r\\n4. A teammate other than the author must approve the policy change. Policy changes, including\\r\\n   threshold changes, follow the same review rules as `docs/engineering-standards.md`.\\r\\n\\r\\nInline V8 ignore comments are discouraged; if one is unavoidable it must carry a reason in the same\\r\\ncomment and be called out in the PR.\\r\\n\\r\\n## 7. Known limitations\\r\\n\\r\\n- V8 records one statement per source line. In files that tests load, comment and blank lines and\\r\\n  type annotations count as covered, which inflates the changed-line percentage slightly.\\r\\n- The gate does not merge Playwright or SQL test coverage.\\r\\n- In CI the PR is checked out as GitHub\u0027s merge commit. The gate diffs that commit against the\\r\\n  merge-base with the base branch, so it measures the PR\u0027s own changes as they will merge.\\r\\n\\r\\n\\r\\n\"}"
